It follows from the properties (
5.59
) of

\Phiit_{(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)}
that this provides a functorial
(two-sided) inverse object with respect to the 1-morphism
\otimes
,
and hence shows that the morphism

(\otimes,\operatorname{pr}_{1})\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})

is an equivalence in
\mathscr{H}
(where the product is taken in
\mathscr{H}
).
Thus
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
is indeed a group object in
\mathscr{H}
.
∎

Theorem 5.63
.

There is a smooth 2-group extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

(5.64)

where we abbreviate
\ast_{\mathscr{H}}={\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
by
1
.

Proof.

The projection functor
\operatorname{pr}\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
is a smooth
2-group via
\otimes_{G}\colon\underline{G}\times_{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}\underline{G}\longrightarrow\underline{G}
defined by

(f_{1}\colon c\longrightarrow G)\otimes_{G}(f_{0}\colon c\longrightarrow G)=(f_{1}\,f_{0}\colon c\longrightarrow G)\ .

(5.65)

It is evident from (
5.61
) that
p\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}
is a morphism of smooth 2-groups.
It is a Grothendieck fibration in groupoids by construction, and it is surjective on objects since
G
is connected (as we have argued at the beginning of this section).

Next we define the morphism
\iota\colon\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
in
\mathscr{H}
.
Over a Cartesian space
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
, it is simply the canonical inclusion

\displaystyle\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{e_{c}}^{*}\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G})\ .

(5.66)

Here
e_{c}\colon c\longrightarrow G
is the constant map at the unit element of
G
.
Since the inclusion of line bundles into morphisms of bundle gerbes strictly maps the tensor product to the composition
[
Wal07b
,
Bun17
]
, we readily find that
\iota
is a morphism of smooth 2-groups.

To see that (
5.64
) is an extension of smooth 2-groups, we first show that the inclusion
\iota
is an equivalence
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow\ker^{\mathtt{h}}(p)
.
By Lemma
A.2
from Appendix
A
and the fact that
p\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}
is a
Grothendieck fibration in groupoids, it follows that the canonical inclusion
\ker(p)\hookrightarrow\ker^{\mathtt{h}}(p)
is an equivalence.
Consequently, it suffices to show that
\iota
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow\ker(p)
.
Over an object
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
, the fibre of
\ker(p)
consists of the automorphism groupoid of
\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)
.
It is well-known
[
Wal07b
]
that the inclusion

\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)\longrightarrow\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)
given by
L\longmapsto L\otimes 1_{\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}}
is an equivalence of groupoids.

To see that the functor
p\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}
is an
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
-principal 2-bundle (see Definition
5.40
), it now suffices to show that the functor

\displaystyle(1,\alpha)\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\underline{G}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})

(5.67)

\displaystyle\big((f,A),L\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big((f,A),(f,A\otimes L)\big)

(5.68)

is an equivalence in
\mathscr{H}
, where we have written out the product in
\mathscr{H}
as the fibre product over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
.
Observe that by the equivalence
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow\ker^{\mathtt{h}}(p)
,
it is enough to consider the action of
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
, and since
\underline{G}

has discrete fibres, i.e. the fibres have no non-identity morphisms,
there is an identity
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\underline{G}}^{\mathtt{h}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})={\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times_{\underline{G}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
, and hence we can work with the strict pullback instead of the homotopy pullback.

Since both sides are fibred over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
, it suffices to show that this functor is an equivalence on all fibres
[
Vis05
, Proposition 3.36]
.
Thus we fix an object
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
.
We need to check that the functor

\displaystyle(1,\alpha)_{|c}\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}\times\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}\times_{\underline{G}_{\,|c}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}

(5.69)

\displaystyle\big((f,A),L\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big((f,A),(f,A\otimes L)\big)

(5.70)

is an equivalence.
By construction, both sides decompose into coproducts

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}\times\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)=\coprod_{f\colon c\longrightarrow G}\,\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\times\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)

(5.71)

and

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}\times_{\underline{G}_{\,|c}}{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}=\coprod_{f\colon c\longrightarrow G}\,\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\times\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\ ,

(5.72)

so the functor
(1,\alpha)_{|c}
decomposes into functors

\displaystyle(1,\alpha)_{|f}\colon\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\times\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)

(5.73)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\times\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)(\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G},\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G})\ .

(5.74)

This functor acts as the identity on the first factor and as the standard action of line bundles on isomorphisms of bundle gerbes in the second factor.
Thus
(1,\alpha)_{|f}
is an equivalence since on any manifold
X
, the category of 1-isomorphisms between any given bundle gerbes is a torsor category over
\mathrm{HLBdl}(X)
with respect to this action
[
Wal07b
]
.
∎

Proposition 5.75
.

If
G
acts non-trivially on
M
, then the extension (
5.64
) is not central.

Proof.

This follows readily from the explicit forms (
5.61
) and (
5.62
) of the product and the inverse in
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
, together with the fact that composition of morphisms of bundle gerbes is compatible with tensoring by line bundles.
Explicitly, given
(f,A)\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}
and
L\in\mathrm{HLBdl}(c\times M)
we find

(f,A)\otimes\iota(L)\otimes(f,A)^{-1}\cong(e_{c},\Phiit_{f}^{*}L)=\iota(\Phiit_{f}^{*}L)\ .

(5.76)

Hence
({\mathrm{Ad}},\alpha)_{c}(f)(L)\cong\Phiit_{f}^{*}L
.
Observe that since
\underline{G}
has discrete fibres, we have
\ker^{\mathtt{h}}(p)=\ker(p)
, and by the equivalence
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow\ker(p)
it is sufficient to consider the adjoint action on the smooth 2-group
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
here.

Let
c=*
, so that the data
f
corresponds to an element
g\in G
.
Assume that the extension (
5.64
) is central.
Then, by Construction
5.48
and Definition
5.56
, there is an isomorphism
\varphi\colon({\mathrm{Ad}},\alpha)_{*}\longrightarrow 1_{\mathrm{HLBdl}(M)}
of morphisms of 2-groups
G\longrightarrow\mathsf{Aut}(\mathrm{HLBdl}(M))
.
Let
I\in\mathrm{HLBdl}(M)
denote the trivial line bundle, and let
\psi\colon I\longrightarrow I
be any automorphism, i.e. any
{\operatorname{U}}(1)
-valued function on
M
.
The naturality of
\varphi
then implies, in particular, that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}I=I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I=g^{*}I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{I,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{I,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

(5.77)

commutes.
But this is equivalent to the identity
\psi=g^{*}\psi
for any
g\in G
and
\psi\colon M\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)
, which is a contradiction if the
G
-action on
M
is non-trivial.
∎

We now obtain an action of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
on
\mathcal{G}
in the following sense:
let
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathscr{H}
denote the category fibred in groupoids
over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
which is defined as follows. Consider the presheaf of groupoids on
\underline{M}
that assigns to
f\colon c\longrightarrow M
the category
\mathrm{BGrb}(c)(\mathcal{I},f^{*}\mathcal{G})
.
Then
q:\widehat{\mathcal{G}}\longrightarrow\underline{M}
is obtained by applying the Grothendieck construction to this presheaf.
The action of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
on
\mathcal{G}
is then the morphism of categories
over
\underline{G\times M}\cong\underline{G}\times\underline{M}
obtained through the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\times\Phiit^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{G}\times\underline{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\times q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Phiit}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\times q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit}

(5.78)

where we suppress pullbacks and denote by
\operatorname{pr}_{M},\Phiit\colon\underline{G}\times\underline{M}\longrightarrow\underline{M}
the functors induced from the smooth maps
\operatorname{pr}_{M},\Phiit
via postcomposition.
The functor
\widehat{\Phiit}
sends an object
({\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{J})\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})_{|c}\times\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{|f}
to the composition

{\Delta}^{*}(1_{c}\times f)^{*}({\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{J})
, where
{\Delta}\colon c\longrightarrow c\times c
is the diagonal map.

The construction
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}
is 2-functorial: let
E\colon\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be a 1-isomorphism of
bundle gerbes. Pick an adjoint inverse
E^{\vee}
.
The 1-isomorphism
E
induces a 1-isomorphism of smooth 2-groups

\displaystyle\widehat{E}\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})

(5.79)

\displaystyle\big(f\colon c\longrightarrow G,A\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\Big(f,\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}E^{\vee}}\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\overset{A}{\longrightarrow}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\Phiit_{f}^{*}E}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\Big)\ .

(5.80)

Let
E,E^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be 1-isomorphisms
and
\eta\colon E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
a 2-isomorphism. We
construct a smooth 2-isomorphism
\widehat{\eta}\colon\widehat{E}\longrightarrow\widehat{E^{\prime}}

whose component at an object
(f,A)
of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ \operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}E^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}{E^{\prime}}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}E^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\!\eta^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}\eta}

(5.81)

5.5
Descent description of the 2-group extension

We can describe the smooth 2-group
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
in a way analogously to Section
3.3
; that is, we can construct
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
in terms of descent data for the path fibration
P_{0}G\longrightarrow G
and the parallel transport on
\mathcal{G}
introduced in Section
4
.
However, for a bundle gerbe
\mathcal{G}
this construction is more involved
compared to the case of a principal bundle
P
.
In particular, we need to replace the associated bundle construction
(P_{0}G\times{\mathrm{Gau}}(P))/{\sim}
of
\mathcal{L}_{G}
(cf. Section
3.3
) by a homotopy-coherent version.
Once established, the descent presentation of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
allows us to study and compute this smooth 2-group very explicitly in certain situations, as we demonstrate in Section
6
.

Recalling the notation of Section
3.3
, let
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M)
be a bundle gerbe with
connection on
M
.
Using the smooth map (
3.38
) we obtain a diffeological hermitean line bundle

{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\coloneqq(L\Phiit)^{*}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow(P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M\ .

(5.82)

This object is completely analogous to (
3.38
) when one views the holonomy of a line bundle
L

on
M
as the transgression of
L
to the loop space
LM
, and subsequently the
transgression line bundle
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
as the holonomy of the bundle gerbe
\mathcal{G}
on
M
(cf. Section
4.4
).
In the adjoint picture, we can interpret
{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}
as a smooth
assignment of a line bundle with connection
{\mathsf{L}}_{(\gamma,\alpha)}

on
M
to each pair of based paths
(\gamma,\alpha)\in(P_{0}G)^{[2]}
.

Consider the simplicial diffeological space
(P_{0}G)^{[\bullet]}\times M
with face maps

d_{i}:(P_{0}G)^{[n]}\times M\longrightarrow(P_{0}G)^{[n-1]}\times M

for
0\leq i\leq n-1
defined by deleting the
i
-th entry of

(P_{0}G)^{[n]}
.
The fusion product
\lambda
on the transgression line bundle
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
over the
loop space
LM
induces an isomorphism

(L\Phiit)^{*}\lambda\colon d_{0}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{2}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\longrightarrow d_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}

(5.83)

of hermitean line bundles over
(P_{0}G)^{[3]}\times M
, which is coherent over
(P_{0}G)^{[4]}\times M
.

Remark 5.84
.

In an adjoint fashion, the hermitean line bundle
{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}
on
(P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
from (
5.82
) can be seen as a morphism of smooth groupoids
{\mathsf{L}}\colon(P_{0}G)^{[2]}\longrightarrow\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
(under the equivalence of diffeological vector bundles and morphisms to
\mathrm{HLBdl}
following from
[
Bun20a
, Thm. 4.8]
).
In this picture, the coherent isomorphism
(L\Phiit)^{*}\lambda
from (
5.83
) corresponds to a coherent isomorphism
d_{0}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}\otimes d_{2}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}\longrightarrow d_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{L}}
of morphisms
(P_{0}G)^{[3]}\longrightarrow\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
.
In this sense,
({\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv},(L\Phiit)^{*}\lambda)
represent a smooth
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
-valued Čech 1-cocycle on
G
with respect to the Čech nerve of the subduction
P_{0}G\longrightarrow G
.
Note that this nicely fits the formalism for higher principal bundles with not-necessarily strict structure groups—such as
\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}
—from
[
NSS15
]
.

\vartriangleleft

Definition 5.85
.

Let
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
be a Cartesian space and
f\colon c\longrightarrow G
a smooth
map. We define a category
{\mathfrak{Des}}^{\mathtt{PSh}}_{\mathsf{L}}(f)
with

•

\underline{\rm Objects:}
pairs
(J,\jmath)
, where
J\in\mathrm{HLBdl}(f^{*}P_{0}G\times M)
and where
\jmath
is an isomorphism of hermitean
line bundles

\jmath\colon d_{1}^{*}J\longrightarrow\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}J

(5.86)

over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
, where
\widehat{f}^{\,[n]}\colon(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[n]}\longrightarrow P_{0}G^{[n]}
is the canonical map induced by the pullback of the subduction
P_{0}G\longrightarrow G
along
f
.
These data are required to satisfy the compatibility condition that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}d_{1}^{*}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}\big((\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}J\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{1}^{*}d_{0}^{*}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{2}^{*}d_{0}^{*}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{1}^{*}d_{0}^{*}J}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}\jmath}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\jmath}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes d_{0}^{*}\jmath}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}^{\,[3]*}\lambda\otimes 1}

(5.87)

is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{HLBdl}((f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[3]}\times M)
, where
we use the simplicial relations.

•

\underline{\rm Morphisms:}
a morphism
(J,\jmath)\longrightarrow(J^{\prime},\jmath^{\prime})
is an isomorphism
\psi\colon J\longrightarrow J^{\prime}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}J^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}J^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\jmath}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes d_{0}^{*}\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\jmath^{\prime}}

(5.88)

is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{HLBdl}((f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M)
.

Pullbacks of morphisms of bundle gerbes turns the assignment
(f\colon c\longrightarrow G)\longmapsto{\mathfrak{Des}}^{\mathtt{PSh}}_{\mathsf{L}}(f)
into a presheaf of groupoids on
\underline{G}\,
.
(This is actually even a sheaf of groupoids, but we will not need this fact here.)
Applying the Grothendieck construction, we obtain a category fibred in
groupoids over
\underline{G}\,
,
p_{\mathsf{L}}:{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}\longrightarrow\underline{G}\,
, and composing with the canonical projection functor
\operatorname{pr}\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
we obtain a category fibred in groupoids over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathsf{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}}

(5.89)

which defines a smooth groupoid
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
.

Proposition 5.90
.

The functor
\pi_{\mathsf{L}}:{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
is a smooth 2-group.

Proof.

Let
(f_{0},J_{0},\jmath_{0}),(f_{1},J_{1},\jmath_{1})\in{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
be two objects, where
(J_{i},\jmath_{i})
lies in the fibre over a smooth map
f_{i}\colon c\longrightarrow G
for
i=0,1
.
The product
(f_{1},J_{1},\jmath_{1})\otimes(f_{0},J_{0},\jmath_{0})
is defined as follows.
First, observe that it should lie in the fibre of
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
over the
pointwise product map
f_{1}\,f_{0}\colon c\longrightarrow G
,
u\longmapsto f_{1}(u)\,f_{0}(u)
.
Consider the smooth map

\displaystyle F\colon m^{*}P_{0}G\times^{\phantom{{\dagger}}}_{G\times G}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}P_{0}G\times^{\phantom{{\dagger}}}_{G\times G}\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}P_{0}G

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{\partial\Delta^{2}}G

(5.91)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{10},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\longmapsto F(\gamma_{10},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{0})=\big(\gamma_{10},\gamma_{1}\,\gamma_{0}(1),\,\gamma_{0}\big)\ ,

(5.92)

where
m\colon G\times G\longrightarrow G
is the multiplication of

G
, and
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{2}
are the projections to the first and
second factors of
G\times G
.
Let us denote by
\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}}
the composition
P_{0}G\times M\longrightarrow G\times M\longrightarrow M
, where the first map evaluates a based path at its end
point and the second map is the action
\Phiit
of
G
on
M
.
The pair
(f_{1},f_{0})
defines a map
c\longrightarrow G\times G
.
Let
{\mathsf{s}}\colon P_{\partial\Delta^{2}}G\longrightarrow LG
be the map defined in (
4.33
), and let (by a slight abuse of notation)
L\Phiit\colon LG\times M\longrightarrow LM
denote the map
(\gamma,x)\longmapsto\gamma_{x}
, with
\gamma_{x}(t)=\Phiit_{\gamma(t)}(x)
.
Consider the hermitean line bundle

K\coloneqq F^{*}{\mathsf{s}}^{*}L\Phiit^{*}\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}\otimes(1_{P_{0}G}\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}J_{1}\otimes J_{0}

(5.93)

on the diffeological space

Y_{f_{1},f_{0}}\coloneqq\big((f_{1}\,f_{0})^{*}P_{0}G\times_{c}f_{1}^{*}P_{0}G\times_{c}f_{0}^{*}P_{0}G\big)\times M\ .

We claim that the bundle
K
descends along the projection
p_{1}\colon Y_{f_{1},f_{0}}\longrightarrow(f_{1}\,f_{0})^{*}P_{0}G\times M
.
The descended bundle is the hermitean line bundle underlying the product
(f_{1},J_{1},\jmath_{1})\otimes(f_{0},J_{0},\jmath_{0})
.

We thus endow the bundle
K
with an isomorphism
\kappa\colon(p_{1})_{0}^{*}K\longrightarrow(p_{1})_{1}^{*}K
over
Y_{f_{1},f_{0}}^{[2]}
, which is required
to satisfy a cocycle relation over
Y_{f_{1},f_{0}}^{[3]}
.
An element of
Y_{f_{1},f_{0}}^{[2]}
can be identified with a pair of triples
((\gamma_{10},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{0}),(\gamma_{10},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}))
, where
(\gamma_{i},\gamma^{\prime}_{i})\in(P_{0}G)^{[2]}
for
i=0,1
.
We define the isomorphism
\kappa
as the composition of the fusion product
\lambda
on
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
with
(1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\jmath_{1}\otimes\jmath_{0}
.
Then the cocycle condition simply follows from the compatibility condition (
5.87
) and the associativity of the fusion product.
(We also need to use thin reparameterisations, but these are
implemented in a completely coherent way by the thin-homotopy invariant connection on
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
.)

Thus we obtain a descended hermitean line bundle
{\mathfrak{Des}}(K,\kappa)
on

(f_{1}\,f_{0})^{*}P_{0}G\times M
(for descent properties of diffeological
vector bundles, see
[
Bun20a
]
).
Applying the fusion product in the first tensor factor of
K
, we obtain an isomorphism which (by the associativity of
\lambda
) descends to an isomorphism

\jmath_{K}\colon d_{1}^{*}{\mathfrak{Des}}(K,\kappa)\longrightarrow\big(\,\widehat{f_{1}\,f_{0}}{}^{[2]}\times 1\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{0}^{*}{\mathfrak{Des}}(K,\kappa)

(5.94)

over
((f_{1}\,f_{0})^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
.
Again by the associativity of
\lambda
and thin-homotopy invariance, the pair
({\mathfrak{Des}}(K,\kappa),\jmath_{K})
satisfies the relation (
5.87
), and hence it makes sense to set

(f_{1},J_{1},\jmath_{1})\otimes(f_{0},J_{0},\jmath_{0})\coloneqq\big(f_{1}\,f_{0},{\mathfrak{Des}}(K,\kappa),\jmath_{K}\big)\ .

(5.95)

The action of the product
\otimes
in
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
on morphisms simply sends
(\psi_{1},\psi_{0})
to the descent along
p_{1}
of the isomorphism
1_{\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}}\otimes(1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\psi_{1}\otimes\psi_{0}
.
The unitors of
\otimes
are readily obtained from the construction, and the associator is defined from the fusion product; its coherence is yet another application of the associativity of
\lambda
and the superficiality of the parallel transport on
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
.
Inverses are constructed analogously to (
5.62
).
Finally, all constructions are compatible with pullbacks along maps
\varphi\colon c^{\prime}\longrightarrow c
of Cartesian spaces, so that we obtain the structure of a smooth 2-group on
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.96
.

There is a weakly commutative diagram of smooth 2-groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psiit}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathsf{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathsf{L}}}

(5.97)

where the functor
\Psiit
is an equivalence.

Proof.

By the functoriality of
\mathcal{G}\longmapsto{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
(see Section
5.4
) we can assume that we are in the case where

is the regression of a transgression, so that we have direct access to our construction of a parallel transport on
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
from Section
4.4
.
We start by constructing the functor
\Psiit
.
For this, we construct a diagram in the 2-category
\mathscr{H}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Des}}\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Hom}_{1}({\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}},\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}

(5.98)

and the functor
\Psiit
is the composition from left to right.
Each of the functors in (
5.98
) is an equivalence of
categories fibred in groupoids over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
, and hence so is
\Psiit
.

For a smooth map
f\colon c\longrightarrow G
, let
\varpi_{f}\colon f^{*}P_{0}G\times M\longrightarrow c\times M
denote the canonical projection.
First we define the category
{\mathfrak{Des}}({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))
.
It is obtained via the Grothendieck construction applied to the
presheaf
{\mathfrak{Des}}^{\mathtt{PSh}}({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))
of groupoids on
\underline{G}\,
, which
assigns to a smooth map
f\colon c\longrightarrow G
the groupoid
{\mathfrak{Des}}^{\mathtt{PSh}}({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))(f)
where

•

objects are pairs
(A,\alpha)
of a 1-isomorphism
A\colon\varpi_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\varpi_{f}^{*}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
over
f^{*}P_{0}G\times M

and a 2-isomorphism
\alpha\colon d_{1}^{*}A\longrightarrow d_{0}^{*}A
over

(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
, which is coherent over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[3]}\times M
, and

•

morphisms
(A,\alpha)\longrightarrow(A^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
are given by
2-isomorphisms
\psi\colon A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
satisfying
\alpha^{\prime}\circ d_{1}^{*}\psi=d_{0}^{*}\psi\circ\alpha
.

The functor
\varpi^{*}
simply pulls back 1-morphisms
A\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
along the subductions
\varpi_{f}
.
This functor is an equivalence since morphisms of bundle gerbes satisfy descent
3
3

3

In
[
Bun17
]
the descent property was proven along surjective submersions of manifolds, but the proof directly carries over to subductions of diffeological spaces.

[
Bun17
, Theorem A.19]
.

Next we introduce some notation:
we define the map

\displaystyle P_{0}\Phiit\colon P_{0}G\times M

\displaystyle\longrightarrow PM

(5.99)

\displaystyle(\gamma,x)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\gamma_{x}\ ,

(5.100)

where

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}(t)=\Phiit_{\gamma(t)}(x)=:P_{0}\Phiit(\gamma,x)(t)

(5.101)

for all
t\in[0,1]
.
Observe that

{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}\circ P_{0}\Phiit=\operatorname{pr}_{M}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}\circ P_{0}\Phiit=\Phiit\circ({\mathrm{ev}}_{1}\times 1_{M})\ .

(5.102)

Thus the pullback of the parallel transport 1-isomorphism
(
4.30
) by the map
P_{0}\Phiit
is a morphism

(P_{0}\Phiit)^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow({\mathrm{ev}}_{1}\times 1_{M})^{*}\Phiit^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}

(5.103)

in
\mathrm{BGrb}(P_{0}G\times M)
.
Given a smooth map

, we obtain a smooth map

P_{0}\Phiit\circ\big(\widehat{f}\times 1_{M}\big)\colon f^{*}P_{0}G\times M\longrightarrow PM\ .

(5.104)

It satisfies

{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}\circ P_{0}\Phiit\circ\big(\widehat{f}\times 1_{M}\big)=\operatorname{pr}_{M}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}\circ P_{0}\Phiit\circ\big(\widehat{f}\times 1_{M}\big)=\Phiit_{f}\circ\varpi_{f}\ ,

(5.105)

where

is the projection.
Hence we obtain a morphism

{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}\coloneqq\big(P_{0}\Phiit\circ(\widehat{f}\times 1)\big)^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}\colon\varpi_{f}^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\varpi_{f}^{*}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\ ,

(5.106)

which is defined over
f^{*}P_{0}G\times M
.
By Proposition
4.94
there is a canonical 2-isomorphism

\big(d_{0}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}\big)^{-1}\circ d_{1}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\big(L\Phiit\circ(\tau\times 1_{M})\circ(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M})\big)^{*}\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}\cong\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M})^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv\,\vee}

(5.107)

of 1-automorphisms of the pullback of
\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
to
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
, where
\tau(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{0})
.
Equivalently, this is a 2-isomorphism

\beta_{f}\colon d_{0}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M}\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{1}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}

(5.108)

of 1-isomorphisms
\varpi_{f}^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\varpi_{f}^{*}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
.

Now we come to the definition of the functor
(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
.
Given an object
(f,J,\jmath)\in{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
, define a morphism of bundle gerbes over

via

J\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}\colon\varpi_{f}^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\varpi_{f}^{*}\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\ .

(5.109)

Using the 2-isomorphism (
5.108
), we obtain a 2-isomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}J\otimes d_{1}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}^{*}J\otimes\big(\widehat{f}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M}\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d_{1}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}^{*}J\otimes d_{0}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\jmath\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\jmath}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes\beta_{f}^{-1}}

(5.110)

over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
.
By construction, the 2-isomorphism
\widehat{\jmath}\,
is coherent over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[3]}\times M
, and thus the pair
(J\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},\widehat{\jmath}\,)
defines a descent datum (with respect to the subduction

) for a 1-isomorphism of bundle gerbes

Analogously, morphisms in
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
give rise to morphisms of descent data as constructed above.
This defines the functor

(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}\colon{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Des}}\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\big)\ .

(5.111)

This is a functor of categories fibred in groupoids over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
by the compatibility of pullbacks of bundles and their morphisms with the tensor product.

Finally, we introduce an inverse functor
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,\big)
for
(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}
.
An object
(f,A,\alpha)\in{\mathfrak{Des}}({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))
consists, in particular, of a 1-isomorphism

of bundle gerbes over
f^{*}P_{0}G\times M
.
Another such morphism is given by
{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f}
.
We can hence use the internal hom-functor

in the 2-category

(see

, Section 3.2]
and also

, Section 2.1]
) to produce a hermitean line bundle

\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},A\big)\in\mathrm{HLBdl}(f^{*}P_{0}G\times M)\ .

(5.112)

This comes with an isomorphism over
(f^{*}P_{0}G)^{[2]}\times M
defined
by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},A\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big(d_{1}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},d_{1}^{*}A\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\widehat{f}{}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M}\big)^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes d^{*}_{0}\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},A\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big((\widehat{f}{}^{\,[2]}\times 1_{M})^{*}{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv\,\vee}\otimes d_{0}^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},d_{0}^{*}A\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Hom}_{1}(\beta_{f}^{-1},\alpha)}

(5.113)

where the 2-isomorphism
\beta_{f}
stems from (
5.108
) and where the lower horizontal isomorphism of line bundles stems from the (categorified) linearity of
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}

[
Bun17
, Theorem 3.63]
.
It follows from the properties of
\alpha
and
\beta_{f}
that

defines an object in
({\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}})_{|f}
.
By mapping a morphism
\psi
in
{\mathfrak{Des}}\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\big)_{|f}
to
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f},\psi\big)
, we obtain a functor

\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}},\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,\big)\colon{\mathfrak{Des}}\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\big)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}

(5.114)

of categories fibred in groupoids over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
.
Again by the linearity of
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}
, it follows straightforwardly that

(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\otimes{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}
and
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,\big)
are mutually inverse functors.

To conclude the proof, we need to check that
\Psiit
is compatible with the monoid structures on
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and on
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
.
For
i=0,1
, let
f_{i}\colon c\longrightarrow G
be smooth maps from
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
to
G
, and consider objects
A_{i}\in{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})_{|f_{i}}
in the fibres over

.
By the explicit construction of the 2-group structure on
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
in the proof of Proposition
5.90
it follows that the hermitean line bundle underlying the object
\Psiit(f_{1},A_{1})\otimes\Psiit(f_{0},A_{0})
of
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
is given as the descent of the bundle

\displaystyle{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes(1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{Hom}_{1}({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f_{1}},\varpi_{f_{1}}^{*}A_{1})\big)\otimes\mathsf{Hom}_{1}({\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{f_{0}},\varpi_{f_{0}}^{*}A_{0})

(5.115)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big((1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\widehat{f}_{1}^{\,*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},(1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\varpi_{f_{1}}^{*}A_{1}\big)\otimes\mathsf{Hom}_{1}(\widehat{f}_{0}^{\,*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},\varpi_{f_{0}}^{*}A_{0})

(5.116)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathsf{L}}^{\dashv}\otimes\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big((1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\widehat{f}_{1}^{\,*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1}\circ\widehat{f}_{0}^{\,*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},\,(1\times\Phiit_{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}})^{*}\varpi_{f_{1}}^{*}A_{1}\circ\varpi_{f_{0}}^{*}A_{0}\big)

(5.117)

\displaystyle\cong\mathsf{Hom}_{1}\big((\widehat{f_{1}\,f_{0}})^{*}{\mathtt{pt}}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{1},\,\varpi_{f_{1}\,f_{0}}^{*}(\Phiit_{f_{0}}^{*}A_{1}\circ A_{0})\big)\ .

(5.118)

The first and second isomorphisms follow from the properties of the
internal hom-functor
\mathsf{Hom}_{1}
.
The third isomorphism is a direct application of Proposition
4.94
.
The bundle in the last line is the line bundle underlying the object

\Psiit\big((f_{1},A_{1})\otimes(f_{0},A_{0})\big)
of
{\mathfrak{Des}}_{\mathsf{L}}
(see (
5.61
)).
Hence the canonical isomorphism (
5.115
) establishes the compatibility of
\Psiit
.
Its coherence again follows from the properties of the transgression line bundle
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{G}
.
The proofs that
\Psiit
respects unitors as well as the weak
commutativity of the diagram (
5.97
) are straightforward.
∎

5.6
Equivariant bundle gerbes

We shall now investigate the relation between sections of the smooth 2-group extension
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}
and equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}
.
We first recall an explicit definition of an equivariant bundle gerbe from
[
GSW11
]
(see also
[
M+17
]
), which can be understood very nicely from the perspective of the formalism developed in
[
NS11
]
.

Let
G
be a connected Lie group,
M
a manifold with
G
-action
\Phiit\colon G\times M\longrightarrow M
, and

\mathcal{G}
a hermitean bundle gerbe over
M
.
Corresponding to the action groupoid
G\times M\rightrightarrows M
there is a simplicial manifold

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\ G^{\times 2}\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\ ,}

(5.119)

with face maps
d_{i}:G^{\times n}\times M\longrightarrow G^{\times n-1}\times M
for
0\leq i\leq n
given by

\displaystyle d_{i}(g_{0},g_{1},\dots,g_{n-1},x)=\begin{cases}\big(g_{0},g_{1},\dots,g_{n-2},\Phiit_{g_{n-1}}(x)\big)&\text{for }i=n\\
(g_{0},g_{1},\dots,g_{i-1}\,g_{i},\dots,g_{n-1},x)&\text{for }0<i<n\\
(g_{1},\dots,g_{n-1},x)&\text{for }i=0\end{cases}\ .

(5.120)

On
G\times M
, the face maps
d_{0}=\operatorname{pr}_{M}
and
d_{1}=\Phiit
are the source and target
maps of the action groupoid.

Definition 5.121
.

Let
G
be a connected Lie group,
M
a manifold with
G
-action
\Phiit\colon G\times M\longrightarrow M
, and
\mathcal{G}
a hermitean bundle gerbe over
M
.
A
G
-equivariant structure
on
\mathcal{G}
consists of a 1-isomorphism
A\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit^{*}\mathcal{G}

over
G\times M

and a 2-isomorphism
\chi\colon d_{2}^{*}A\circ d_{0}^{*}A\longrightarrow d_{1}^{*}A
over
G^{\times 2}\times M
such that

d_{2}^{*}\chi\circ\big(1_{(d_{3}\circ d_{2})^{*}A}\circ d_{0}^{*}\chi\big)=d_{1}^{*}\chi\circ\big(1_{(d_{0}\circ d_{0})^{*}A}\circ d_{3}^{*}\chi\big)

(5.122)

over
G^{\times 3}\times M
. A
morphism

(A,\chi)\longrightarrow(A^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
between equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}

consists of a 2-isomorphism
\vartheta\colon A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}

such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}A\circ d_{0}^{*}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}A^{\prime}\circ d_{0}^{*}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}A^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}^{*}\vartheta\circ d_{0}^{*}\vartheta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}^{*}\vartheta}

(5.123)

commutes.
We denote by
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G})
the groupoid of equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}
.

A
splitting
of
p\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}
is a smooth 2-group homomorphism

s\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
such that
p\circ s=1_{\underline{G}}
. We assume here
for simplicity and without loss of generality that unitors are strictly preserved. We denote by
\mathcal{S}(\underline{G}\,;{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}))
the
groupoid of splittings of
p\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}\,
.
Concretely, a splitting consists of

•

a 1-isomorphism
s(f)\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}

of bundle gerbes on
c\times M
for every
Cartesian space
c\in{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
and sections
f\in G(c)
,

•

a 2-isomorphism
s(\varphi)\colon s(f)\longrightarrow\varphi^{*}s(f^{\prime})
for every morphism
\varphi\colon f\longrightarrow f^{\prime}
in
\underline{G}
, and

•

a 2-isomorphism

s(f)\otimes s(f^{\prime})\longrightarrow s(f\,f^{\prime})
in
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
for every
f,f^{\prime}\in G(c)
,

such that
\varphi^{*}s(\varphi^{\prime})\circ s(\varphi)=s(\varphi^{\prime}\circ\varphi)
and the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f)\otimes s(f^{\prime})\otimes s(f^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f)\otimes s(f^{\prime}\,f^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f\,f^{\prime})\otimes s(f^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f\,f^{\prime}\,f^{\prime\prime})}

(5.124)

commutes.
A morphism
\omega\colon s\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
of splittings consists
of 2-isomorphisms
\omega(f)\colon s(f)\longrightarrow s^{\prime}(f)
in

\mathrm{BGrb}(c\times M)
for all
f\in G(c)
such that the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}s(f^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}s^{\prime}(f^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\omega(f^{\prime})}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f)\otimes s(f^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}(f)\otimes s^{\prime}(f^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f\,f^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}(f\,f^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega(f)\otimes\omega(f^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega(f\,f^{\prime})}

(5.125)

commute.

In what follows we
construct an equivalence

\displaystyle\Xi\colon\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}\big(\underline{G}\,;{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\big)

(5.126)

of categories.
Let
(A,\chi)
be an equivariant structure on
\mathcal{G}
and
(f\colon c\longrightarrow G)\in G(c)
. Pulling back
A
along
\Phiit_{f}
gives rise to a 1-isomorphism
\Phiit_{f}^{*}A\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}
over
c\times M
. We can define the section
\Xi(A,\chi)\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})

by
\Xi(A,\chi)(f)=\Phiit_{f}^{*}A\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}
. The 2-isomorphisms
\Xi(A,\chi)(\varphi)

are induced by general properties of pullbacks and the 2-isomorphism
\chi
induces the 2-isomorphism encoding the
compatibility with multiplication.
The action of
\Xi
on morphisms of equivariant structures is again by pullback along
\Phiit_{f}
.

Theorem 5.127
.

The functor
\Xi\colon\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}(\underline{G}\,;{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}))
is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

We start by showing that
\Xi
is essentially surjective.
Let
s\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
be a splitting.
We pick a good open cover
\{c_{i}\}_{i\in\Lambda}
of
G
. This
induces good open covers of
G^{\times 2}
and
G^{\times 3}
given by
\{c_{i}\times c_{j}\}_{i,j\in\Lambda}
and
\{c_{i}\times c_{j}\times c_{k}\}_{i,j,k\in\Lambda}
, respectively. Every
c_{i}
comes with an embedding
f_{i}\colon c_{i}\longrightarrow G
and hence can be regarded as an
object of
\underline{G}\,
. Applying the section
s\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
to all elements of the open cover
provides a collection of compatible 1-isomorphisms

s(c_{i})\coloneqq s(f_{i}\colon c_{i}\longrightarrow G)\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit_{f_{i}}^{*}\mathcal{G}\ .

On double intersections
c_{ij}\coloneqq c_{i}\cap c_{j}
we get coherent
2-isomorphisms
4
4

4

Here we interpret
s
as a map of stacks, i.e. a natural transformation of 2-functors
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}\longrightarrow\operatorname{\mathscr{C}at}
, via the
inverse Grothendieck construction.

\displaystyle s(c_{ij})\colon s(c_{i})_{|c_{ij}}\longrightarrow s(f_{i}{}_{|c_{ij}})=s(f_{j}{}_{|c_{ij}})\longrightarrow s(c_{j})_{|c_{ij}}\ ,

(5.128)

since

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
is a Grothendieck fibration.
Hence the 1-isomorphisms
s(c_{i})

glue together to a 1-isomorphism
A_{s}\colon\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\Phiit^{*}\mathcal{G}
over
G\times M
.

Let
\operatorname{pr}_{1},\operatorname{pr}_{2}:G^{\times 2}\longrightarrow G
be the projections to the first and
second factors, and
m:G^{\times 2}\longrightarrow G
the multiplication in
G
.
From
A_{s}
we can construct three 1-morphisms
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}A_{s}
,
\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}A_{s}
, and
m^{*}A_{s}
over
G^{\times 2}\times M
.
We would like to show that these 1-morphisms are canonically isomorphic to the 1-morphisms constructed from the
good open cover
\{c_{i}\times c_{j}\}_{i,j\in\Lambda}
by applying
s

to the morphisms
\operatorname{pr}_{1|c_{i}\times c_{j}}
,
\operatorname{pr}_{2|c_{i}\times c_{j}}
, and
m_{|c_{i}\times c_{j}}
on
{c_{i}\times c_{j}}\longrightarrow G
, respectively. For this, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{i}\times c_{j}\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{i}\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\times 2}\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{1}}

(5.129)

which implies that

\operatorname{pr}_{1|c_{i}\times c_{j}}^{*}A_{s}=\operatorname{pr}_{1|c_{i}\times c_{j}}^{*}s(c_{i}\hookrightarrow G)\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }s\big(c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}c_{i}\hookrightarrow G\big)\ ,

where the 2-isomorphism is part of the data of the section
s
. The same argument
shows the claim for
\operatorname{pr}_{2}
. To show the corresponding statement for
m
we need to pick a
refinement
\{\widetilde{c}_{a}\}_{a\in\widetilde{\Lambda}}
of the cover
\{c_{i}\times c_{j}\}_{i,j\in\Lambda}

such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\limits_{a\in\widetilde{\Lambda}}\,\widetilde{c}_{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\limits_{i,j\in\Lambda}\,c_{i}\times c_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\limits_{i\in\Lambda}\,c_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\times 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

(5.130)

commutes. The cover
\{\widetilde{c}_{a}\}_{a\in\widetilde{\Lambda}}
can be constructed by choosing a
common refinement of the covers
\{c_{i}\times c_{j}\}_{i,j\in\Lambda}

and
\{m^{-1}(c_{i})\}_{i\in\Lambda}
of
G^{\times 2}
.

The multiplication of
c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}G
with
c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{2}}G
in
\underline{G}
is
c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\ m\ }G
. The structure of a smooth 2-group homomorphism on
s
now provides natural 2-isomorphisms

s\big(c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{pr}_{1}\ }G\big)\otimes s\big(c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{pr}_{2}\ }G\big)\longrightarrow s\big(c_{i}\times c_{j}\xrightarrow{\ m\ }G\big)

(5.131)

which glue together to a 2-isomorphism

\chi_{s}\colon\Phiit^{*}(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}A_{s})\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}A_{s})\longrightarrow m^{*}A_{s}
over
G^{\times 2}\times M
because (
5.131
) is a 2-isomorphism of smooth 1-isomorphisms.
The coherence condition for
\chi_{s}
over
G^{\times 3}\times M
follows from the observation that the various pullbacks to

G^{\times 3}\times M
can be constructed by applying
s
to different functions from
c_{i}\times c_{j}\times c_{k}
to
G
and the coherence condition
for
s
.
This shows that
\Xi
is essentially surjective.

We next show that the functor
\Xi
is faithful: let
\vartheta,\vartheta^{\prime}\colon(A,\chi)\longrightarrow(A^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be isomorphisms
of equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}
such that

\Xi(\vartheta^{\prime})=\Xi(\vartheta)
, and let
g\in G
.
We can take
c=\mathbb{R}^{0}
and
f\colon c\longrightarrow G
to be the constant map at
g
to
conclude that
\varphi_{|\{g\}\times M}=\Xi(A,\chi)(\varphi)(f)
and
\varphi_{|\{g\}\times M}=\Xi(A^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})(\varphi)(f)

agree. Hence the two isomorphisms agree pointwise and the statement follows.

Finally, we show that the functor
\Xi
is full: let
(A,\chi)
and
(A^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}
and

\omega\colon\Xi(A,\chi)\longrightarrow\Xi(A^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
a morphism of splittings.
Evaluating
\omega
on the good open cover
\{c_{i}\}_{i\in\Lambda}
from above provides
isomorphisms
\omega\colon A_{|c_{i}\times M}\longrightarrow A^{\prime}_{|c_{i}\times M}
.
Since
\omega
is a morphism of splittings, these morphisms glue together to a
2-isomorphism
\vartheta_{\omega}\colon A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
. That this is an isomorphism
of equivariant structures follows from the coherence conditions for
\omega
and
the observation that it suffices to check the conditions locally.
∎

Corollary 5.132
.

A bundle gerbe
\mathcal{G}
on a manifold
M
admits an equivariant structure if and only if the 2-group extension

1\longrightarrow\mathrm{HLBdl}^{M}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}\longrightarrow 1

admits a splitting.

.

(5.135)

Being an equivariant 1-isomorphism is a structure and not a property: given a 1-morphism
E\colon\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
of bundle gerbes there is a
set
\mathcal{E}(E)
of equivariant structures on
E
.
According to Theorem
5.127
we can describe the
equivariant structures on
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
by splittings

s\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
and
s^{\prime}\colon\underline{G}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.
We shall now give a description of an equivariant structure on

E
using these homomorphisms of 2-groups.
For this, recall from the end of Section
5.4
that any 1-isomorphism
E\colon\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{BGrb}(M)
gives rise to a morphism of smooth 2-groups
\widehat{E}\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
: choose an adjoint inverse
E^{\vee}
for
E
and define
\widehat{E}
via

(f,A)\longmapsto\Phiit_{f}^{*}E\circ(f,A)\circ E^{\vee}\ .

(5.136)

Proposition 5.137
.

There is a natural bijection
\Xi_{E}
between the set
\mathcal{E}(E)
of equivariant structures
on
E
and the set of 2-isomorphisms
\widehat{\gamma}\colon\widehat{E}\circ s\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
of smooth morphisms of 2-groups.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be
G
-equivariant bundle gerbes on a manifold

M
with a smooth
G
-action
\Phiit\colon G\times M\longrightarrow M
, and let
(E,\gamma)\colon\mathcal{G}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be a 1-isomorphism of equivariant bundle gerbes. Fix an adjoint inverse
E^{\vee}
to
E
.
We construct the 2-isomorphism
\Xi_{E}(\gamma)\colon\widehat{E}\circ s\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
as follows: let

f\colon c\longrightarrow G
be an element of
\underline{G}\,
. The natural transformation
\Xi_{E}(\gamma)

consists of a 2-isomorphism
\Xi_{E}(\gamma)_{f}\colon\widehat{E}\circ s(f)\longrightarrow s^{\prime}(f)

which we construct by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phiit_{f}^{*}E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1\times f)^{*}\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{M}^{*}E^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}(f)}

(5.138)

Using the same methods as in the proof of Theorem
5.127
one can show that
\Xi_{E}
is
a bijection.
∎

Definition 5.139
.

An
equivariant 2-isomorphism
of
G
-equivariant bundle gerbes
(E,\gamma)\longrightarrow(E^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
consists
of a 2-morphism
\eta\colon E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
such that there is
an equality of diagrams

(5.140)

Being an equivariant 2-morphism is a property.

The 2-group extension
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}

can also be used to study the existence of equivariant structures on 1-morphisms.
A condition for 2-isomorphisms of bundle
gerbes to be equivariant is

Proposition 5.141
.

Let
(E,\gamma)
and
(E^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
be equivariant 1-isomorphisms. A 2-isomorphism
\eta\colon E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
is equivariant if and only if
\Xi_{E^{\prime}}(\gamma^{\prime})\circ(\widehat{\eta}\circ 1_{\gamma})=\Xi_{E}(\gamma)
.

Proof.

This follows from Definition
5.139
using the fact that the inverses
E^{\vee}

and
E^{\prime}{}^{\vee}
are adjoints to
E
and
E^{\prime}
.
∎

6
Application I: Nonassociative magnetic translations

Nonassociativity in quantum mechanics has a long history dating back
to foundational work on the theory in the 1930’s. Its interest was revived in the
1980’s with the realisation that the magnetic translation operators
on the states of a charged particle moving in a magnetic monopole
background generally form a nonassociative
algebra
[
Jac85
,
GZ86
]
;
see
[
Sza19a
]
for a mathematical introduction to the subject
together with a survey and comparison of the various approaches to the
quantisation of the pertinent twisted Poisson structures. The recent revived interest in these
models has come about from their conjectural relevance to the
low-energy dynamics
of closed strings in non-geometric
backgrounds,
which are based on arguments invoking T-duality applied to target spaces that are tori or more generally
total spaces of torus bundles
[
Lüs10
,
MSS12
,
BL14
,
MSS14
]
, and other compact Lie groups
[
BP11
]
. See
e.g.
[
Sza19b
]
for a contemporary introduction to the
subject with further references.

As a first application of the general framework presented in this paper, we reformulate the
well-known theory of magnetic translations for source-free magnetic fields in the language of Section
3
.
We then use the results of Section
5
to describe nonassociative
magnetic translations, which were first studied from a geometric
perspective in
[
BMS19
]
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. They were subsequently
studied from a quantum field theory perspective in
[
Mic19
]
,
where generalisations from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to connected Lie groups are also considered. Here we will show that they are induced by a natural section of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{G}(\mathcal{G})\longrightarrow\underline{G}

constructed using the parallel transport of Section
4
.

6.1
Magnetic translations on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

Magnetic translations appear in the quantum mechanics of an
electrically charged particle moving
on a manifold
M
in the presence of a magnetic
field, which is given by a 2-form
B\in\Omega^{2}(M)
. In the
semi-classical Maxwell theory of electromagnetism, the 2-form
B
is
closed and has integer periods. The first requirement
H=\mathrm{d}B=0
is
the statement that there are no magnetic monopoles. The second
requirement is the Dirac charge quantisation condition which states that

B
is the curvature of a connection on a hermitean line bundle
L

over
M
. In Bloch theory (see e.g.
[
Gru00
]
), the line bundle
L
is used in geometric
quantisation of the shift of the canonical symplectic structure on the
cotangent bundle
T^{*}M
by the 2-form
B
, so that the quantum Hilbert
space of wavefunctions for the particle is
\mathcal{H}={\rm L}^{2}(M;L)
, the space of square-integrable
sections of
L
. The (global) symmetry group
G
of the particle acts on
M
, and one would like to promote the
G
-action
to an action on the Hilbert space by linear operators. In quantum
mechanics, this action on
\mathcal{H}
is only required to define a
projective representation of
G
. If
G
acts via translations the resulting operators are called

magnetic translations
. The construction in Section
3
provides
a universal mechanism to construct magnetic translations,
which we will illustrate on the example of a

d
-dimensional torus
M={\mathbb{T}}^{d}
. Magnetic translations on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

have been studied in e.g.
[
Fio13
,
DGTS20
]
(for constant magnetic
fields
B
), but our treatment is more general and also fits in with
expectations from string theory.

Instead of working on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
directly, we work equivariantly on the
universal cover

\mathbb{R}^{d}
by viewing
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{d}/\mathbb{Z}^{d}
as the quotient of the
natural free action
\tau
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
of the discrete subgroup

\mathbb{Z}^{d}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
by translations. The corresponding projection
\pi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

is a surjective submersion. To describe line bundles on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
we
consider the diagram of manifolds

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{U}}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cong\mathbb{R}^{d}\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\mathbb{R}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

(6.1)

where we use the identification
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ni(x,i)\longmapsto(x,x+i)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\mathbb{R}^{d}
; under this identification,

\pi_{0}=\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is the projection and
\pi_{1}=\tau
is the

\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-action on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Any line bundle on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
can be described by a smooth function
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)
satisfying

f(x+i,j)\,f(x,i)=f(x,i+j)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
This means that
f
is a 1-cocycle on the group
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with values in the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-module

C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))
.
We will use the notation
f_{i}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,)\coloneqq f(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,,i)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Concretely, the
{\operatorname{U}}(1)
-bundle described by
f
is the quotient

\displaystyle P_{f}\coloneqq\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\operatorname{U}}(1)\big)\big/{\sim}

(6.2)

by the equivalence relation

\displaystyle(x+i,1)\sim\big(x,f_{i}(x)\big)

(6.3)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Sections of the associated line
bundle
L_{f}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
are in one-to-one correspondence
with equivariant functions on the universal covering space, which are
functions
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{C})
satisfying quasi-periodic boundary conditions

\displaystyle\psi(x+i)=f_{i}(x)\,\psi(x)\ .

(6.4)

The action of the translation group
G=\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

x\longmapsto x+v
, induces
an action
\tau
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
. For
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
, the
bundle
\tau_{v}^{*}P_{f}
is described by the functions
\tau_{v}^{*}f_{i}=f_{i}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,-v)
.
5
5

5

Note that the functions
f_{i}
are not
invariant under the subgroup
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathtt{t}}^{d}\subset\mathbb{R}_{\mathtt{t}}^{d}
, whereas the bundles
P_{f}

and
P_{\tau_{v}^{*}f}
are canonically isomorphic. This is nothing but a concrete
implementation of the fact that pullbacks are only well defined up to canonical isomorphism.
This allows us to give a concrete
description for the fibres of the principal bundle
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
as

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})_{|v}={\mathrm{Bun}}_{{\operatorname{U}}(1)}(M)(P_{f},P_{\tau_{v}^{*}f})=\big\{g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},{\operatorname{U}}(1))\ \big|\ g(x+i)=f_{i}(x)\,f_{i}(x-v)^{-1}\,g(x)\big\}\ .

(6.5)

The group
6
6

6

For an
action of a group
G
on a set
X
, we denote by
X^{G}\subseteq X
the subset of
G
-invariants.

C^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))=C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))^{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}

acts freely and transitively on

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})_{|v}
by pointwise multiplication. The multiplicative structure from
Proposition
3.17
on

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})
takes the concrete form

\displaystyle\mu\big((g,v),(g^{\prime},v^{\prime})\big)\coloneqq\big((\tau_{v^{\prime}}^{*}g)\,g^{\prime},v+v^{\prime}\big)\ .

(6.6)

A smooth section of the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 1\longrightarrow C^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d},{\operatorname{U}}(1))\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}\longrightarrow 1

(6.7)

induces a twisted action of
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
on the quantum
Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}={\rm L}^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d};L_{f})
; here we do not
require this section to be a group homomorphism, and the 2-cocycle twisting this action takes values
in
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))
. We can construct such a section
from the choice of a connection on
P_{f}
, which reproduces the
usual expression for magnetic translations. A connection on
P_{f}
can be described
by a 1-form
A\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{d}\log f=\pi_{0}^{*}A-\pi_{1}^{*}A\ .

(6.8)

This condition implies that the closed 2-form
\mathrm{d}A=\pi^{*}B
descends
to a well-defined magnetic field
B
on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.
The section corresponding to
A
is given by parallel transport:

\displaystyle s_{A}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})

(6.9)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\longmapsto s_{A}(v)=\exp\Big(-\mathrm{i}\,\int_{\Delta^{1}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,;v)}\,A\Big)\ ,

(6.10)

where

\Delta^{1}(x;v)=\{x-v+t\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid 0\leq t\leq 1\}\ .

We check that this is indeed an element of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\tt t}}(P_{f})_{|v}
:

\displaystyle\big(s_{A}(v)\big)(x+i)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(-\mathrm{i}\,\int_{\Delta^{1}(x+i;v)}\,A+\mathrm{i}\,\int_{\Delta^{1}(x;v)}\,A\Big)\ \big(s_{A}(v)\big)(x)

(6.11)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\int_{\Delta^{1}(x;v)}\,\mathrm{d}\log f_{i}\Big)\ \big(s_{A}(v)\big)(x)

(6.12)

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x)\,f_{i}(x-v)^{-1}\ \big(s_{A}(v)\big)(x)\ .

(6.13)

The corresponding 2-cocycle describing the extension agrees with the 2-cocycle constructed
in e.g.
[
Sol18
,
BMS19
]
.

By Proposition
3.20
the extension

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(P_{f})

acts on the total space of the line bundle
L_{f}

and hence on the quantum state space
\mathcal{H}
. The section
s_{A}
realises
translations
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
as linear operators

\mathcal{P}(v):\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}
on this Hilbert space via

\mathcal{P}(v)\psi=s_{A}(v)\,\tau_{v}^{*}\psi\ .

One easily checks that
\mathcal{P}(v)\psi\in\mathcal{H}
for
\psi\in\mathcal{H}
,
i.e.
\big(\mathcal{P}(v)\psi\big)(x+i)=f_{i}(x)\,\big(\mathcal{P}(v)\psi\big)(x)
.
However, they only provide a projective representation of the
translation group
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}

since
s_{A}
is not a group homomorphism. Explicitly, using Stokes’
Theorem we find that the magnetic translations satisfy the relations
of the twisted group algebra

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(v)\,\mathcal{P}(v^{\prime})=\exp\Big(-\mathrm{i}\,\int_{\Delta^{2}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,;v^{\prime},v)}\,\pi^{*}B\Big)\ \mathcal{P}(v+v^{\prime})\ ,

(6.14)

where

\Delta^{2}(x;v^{\prime},v)=\{x-v-v^{\prime}+t_{1}\,v^{\prime}+t_{2}\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid 0\leq t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq 1\}\ ,

and we used the relation

\partial\Delta^{2}(x;v^{\prime},v)=\Delta^{1}(x;v)-\Delta^{1}(x;v+v^{\prime})+\Delta^{1}(x+v;x+v+v^{\prime})

in the simplicial complex in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Remark 6.15
.

By dropping the (quasi-)periodicity conditions everywhere one gets
back the description of magnetic translations
corresponding to (necessarily trivialisable) line bundles over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(cf.
[
BMS19
]
).

\vartriangleleft

6.2
Nonassociative magnetic translations from parallel transport

Dirac’s extension of the classical Maxwell theory assumes a singular magnetic
field
B
whose 3-form curvature
H=\mathrm{d}B
is distributional, with
zero-dimensional support consisting of the locations of magnetic
monopoles on the configuration manifold
M
. However, in applications
to string theory the closed 3-form
H
corresponds to an NS–NS flux and is typically
smooth, as we now assume. The framework described in Section
6.1
is not capable of encoding
magnetic fields with non-vanishing magnetic charge
H=\mathrm{d}B
, since in this
case
B
can never be realised as the curvature of a line bundle. The
quantisation problem now concerns an
H
-twisted Poisson structure
on the cotangent bundle
T^{*}M

[
Sza19a
]
, with twisting
of the canonical Poisson structure which spoils the Jacobi identity
for functions in
C^{\infty}(T^{*}M;\mathbb{C})
that vary along the vertical directions. The
corresponding quantum operators do not associate; it is not
possible to realise a nonassociative algebra by linear operators acting on a Hilbert
space. Different approaches to describing
the nonassociative quantum mechanics of charged particles moving in the background
of a magnetic field with smooth monopole sources are described in
[
MSS14
,
BBBS15
,
KS18
]
.

Fluxes in string theory obey a generalised version of Dirac charge
quantisation (see e.g.
[
Sza13
]
); in particular, the closed
3-form
H
has integer periods and hence is the
curvature of a connection on a hermitean bundle gerbe over
M
.
Based on this observation, in
[
BMS19
]
we suggested
the following approach: geometrically the magnetic field
B

can be interpreted as the curving on a trivial gerbe
\mathcal{I}_{B}
with curvature
H
.
We proposed to use the 2-Hilbert space of sections of
\mathcal{I}_{B}
as a replacement
for the usual Hilbert space of quantum mechanics.
The category of sections of a gerbe
\mathcal{G}
on a manifold
M
is the morphism category

\Gamma(M;\mathcal{G})\coloneqq\mathrm{BGrb}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{G})
; for details on the 2-Hilbert space structure on this category we refer to
[
BSS18
,
BS17
,
Bun17
]
.
As evidence for our approach we constructed a projective
action of nonassociative magnetic translation operators on this 2-Hilbert space,
which naturally encodes the relations of the
H
-twisted Poisson
algebra on
T^{*}M
. However, the drawback of this approach is that it does not work for
topologically non-trivial fluxes
H
, or equivalently for
gerbes
\mathcal{G}
with non-torsion Dixmier-Douady class. Extending our geometric approach to nonassociative
quantum mechanics along these lines was in fact one of our original motivations behind
the present paper.

Let us first explain how the action of nonassociative magnetic translations for magnetic
fields with sources on
M=\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which was described
in
[
BMS19
]
, can be constructed via the 2-group extensions
from Section
5
.
Every gerbe on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is isomorphic to a trivial gerbe

\mathcal{I}_{B}
represented by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

(6.16)

with trivial connection
A=0
and curving
B\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
The connection on
\mathcal{I}_{B}
induces a section
7
7

7

To
simplify the presentation, in the
following we disregard smooth structures and work in the 2-category of
2-groups
2{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rp}}({\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rpd}})
. The extensions to categories fibred
in groupoids over
{\operatorname{\mathscr{C}art}}
is straightfoward.

s_{B}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}\longrightarrow\text{Sym}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{I})

via parallel transport:

\displaystyle s_{B}(v)\coloneqq\big({\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{B}}\big)_{|\Delta^{1}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,;v)}\colon\mathcal{I}_{B}\longrightarrow\tau_{v}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{B}\ .

(6.17)

Combining
s_{B}
with the action of
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{I})
on
\mathcal{I}
induces a higher projective action of

\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
on
\Gamma(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{I})
. We refer to
[
BMS19
, Section 4]
for precise definitions.

Since all line
bundles over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
are isomorphic to a trivial line bundle, the
category
\text{Sym}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{I})_{|v}
at
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
is equivalent to
the category with one object and morphisms
described by smooth functions
\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)
. Thus

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{I})\cong\big(*\big/\hskip-4.26773pt\big/C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))\big)\rtimes\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}\ .

(6.18)

The 2-group structure on
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{I})
is given by

\displaystyle v\otimes v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq v+v^{\prime}\ ,

(6.19)

\displaystyle(h,v)\otimes(h^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big((\tau_{v^{\prime}}^{*}h)\,h^{\prime},v+v^{\prime}\big)\ .

(6.20)

However, under this equivalence the action of the magnetic translation operators
becomes elusive. In
[
BMS19
]
we equipped the 1-morphisms
with a connection to circumvent this problem. We did not require the 2-morphisms to preserve these connections
leading to equivalent categories. The parallel transport 1-morphisms
{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{B}}
can be equipped with
such a connection in a canonical way. This reproduces the constructions from
[
BMS19
]
.

For this, let
\mathcal{I}_{B}
be a trivial bundle gerbe
on a smooth manifold
M
with curving
B\in\Omega^{2}(M)
and curvature
H=\mathrm{d}B
.
We fix a base point
x\in M
. Via transgression and regression
we get a bundle gerbe
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})
defined over the
diffeological path fibration

P_{0}M\longrightarrow M
.
The corresponding line bundle over
(P_{0}M)^{[2]}
admits a canonical trivialisation.
It comes equipped with
a connection 1-form given as the pullback of the transgression of
B
to
the loop space
LM
along the embedding
l\colon(P_{0}M)^{[2]}\longrightarrow LM
.
To describe the curving of
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})
we note that a tangent vector
to a based path
\gamma\in P_{0}M
is a smooth section
\mathcal{V}\in\Gamma([0,1];\gamma^{*}TM)

which is zero in a neighbourhood of
0
and constant in a neighbourhood of
1
.
The 2-form
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(B)\in\Omega^{2}(P_{0}M)
is defined by the transgression formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(B)(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})=\int_{\gamma}\,\imath_{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}\imath_{\mathcal{V}}H\ ,

(6.21)

where
\imath
denotes the interior multiplication between a vector
and a form.
There is a natural 1-isomorphism
[
Wal16
, Section 6.1]

W\colon\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{B}
with
underlying diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{0}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{0}{M})^{[2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{0}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}

(6.22)

of diffeological spaces. The line bundle which is part of this

1
-morphism has a canonical trivialisation for trivial bundle gerbes
\mathcal{I}_{B}

and has the connection 1-form
A_{W}\in\Omega^{1}(P_{0}M)
defined
by the transgression formula

\displaystyle A_{W}(\mathcal{V})=\int_{\gamma}\,\imath_{\mathcal{V}}B\ .

(6.23)

In order to describe the parallel transport, we pull everything back to the path space

PM
along the two evaluation maps
{\mathrm{ev}}_{0},{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}\colon PM\longrightarrow M
.
The parallel transport defined in Section
4
is a 1-morphism

\displaystyle{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})}\colon{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}^{*}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})

(6.24)

given by
a line bundle with connection over the space
P_{\partial\Delta^{2}}M
.
An element of
P_{\partial\Delta^{2}}M

is a triple of paths
(\gamma_{xy},\gamma_{yz},\gamma_{xz})
, where
\gamma_{xy}
is a path
from the base point
x
to some other point
y\in M
,
\gamma_{yz}
is a path from
y
to
a third point
z\in M
, and
\gamma_{xz}
is a path from
x
to
z
.
Again, the line bundle for the parallel transport is trivial since we work
with a trivial bundle gerbe. Its connection is given by

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{\gamma_{xz}}\star\gamma_{yz}\star\gamma_{xy}}\,\imath_{\bullet}B\ ,

(6.25)

where the notation means that the evaluation on a tangent vector
\mathcal{V}
is
given by

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{\gamma_{xz}}\star\gamma_{yz}\star\gamma_{xy}}\,\imath_{\mathcal{V}}B\ .

(6.26)

We obtain a 1-morphism

\displaystyle{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{B}}\colon{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{B}\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}^{*}W^{-1}}{\mathrm{ev}}_{0}^{*}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{pt}}_{1}^{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})}}{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{I}_{B})\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}^{*}W}{\mathrm{ev}}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{B}\ ,

(6.27)

representing the colimit from Definition
4.87
.
Concretely, this is a trivial line bundle over
PM
with connection

\int_{\gamma_{yz}}\,\imath_{\bullet}B\ .

This is exactly the formula used for the magnetic translations
in
[
BMS19
]
in the case
M=\mathbb{R}^{d}
, hence it provides a conceptual underpinning
of the constructions
in
[
BMS19
]
, and moreover generalises them to trivial bundle gerbes on arbitrary
manifolds
M
.

6.3
Nonassociative magnetic translations on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

Now we generalise the description of nonassociative magnetic translations to the
d
-dimensional
torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, see also
[
Mic19
]
for a discussion from a
quantum field theory point of view.
A problem in this context is that for topologically non-trivial gerbes on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, there
are no non-trivial sections. This makes the 2-Hilbert space of sections an uninteresting object
to study.
8
8

8

We expect that there exists a better definition of
sections circumventing this problem.
However, our 2-group extension still exists and should encode
the geometry of nonassociative magnetic translations in this context,
regardless of whether or not sections exist. Non-trivial gerbes over
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
are similarly
treated as coming from
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-equivariant (topologically trivial) gerbes over

\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as in e.g.
[
MW16
, Section 7.1]
.

Bundle gerbes on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
can be described using the surjective submersion

\pi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
and the corresponding
diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0,1}^{*}L\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0,2}^{*}L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

(6.28)

Here we used the identification
(x,i,j)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\longmapsto(x,x+i,x+i+j)\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{[3]}
.
Concretely, a bundle gerbe consists of a line bundle
L

over
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, which we can assume to be trivial without loss of generality, and an isomorphism

f\colon\pi_{0,1}^{*}L\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}L\longrightarrow\pi_{0,2}^{*}L
of line bundles over
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}

satisfying a coherence condition over
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{\times 3}
. We can describe this
isomorphism by a collection of smooth maps
f_{i,j}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and the coherence
condition translates to

f_{i,j}(x)\,f_{i+j,k}(x)=f_{i,j+k}(x)\,f_{j,k}(x+i)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, which is
the 2-cocycle condition for

f_{\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,,\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,}\in{\rm C}^{2}\big(\mathbb{Z}^{d};C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};{\operatorname{U}}(1))\big)\ .

We denote the gerbe described by
f

as
\mathcal{G}_{f}
.

For
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}
, the pullback of
\mathcal{G}_{f}
along the translation

\tau_{v}
can be described by the
2-cocycle
\tau_{v}^{*}f_{i,j}=f_{i,j}(\,\boldsymbol{\cdot}\,-v)
.
Using

[
Bun17
, Proposition A.31]
we can describe the category

{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{G}_{f})_{|v}
at
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\tt t}

up to equivalence in the following way:
its objects are functions

g\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)
satisfying

\displaystyle f_{i,j}(x-v)\,g_{i}(x)\,g_{j}(x+i)=g_{i+j}(x)\,f_{i,j}(x)\ ,

(6.29)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
It is straightforward to deduce the morphisms in
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{G}_{f})

from
[
Bun17
, Proposition A.31]
; we find that a morphism
from
g
to
g^{\prime}
is described by a function
h\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{U}}(1)

satisfying

\displaystyle h(x)\,g_{i}(x)=g_{i}^{\prime}(x)\,h(x+i)

(6.30)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Note that for the trivial 2-cocycle
f_{i,j}=1
this describes the
category of line bundles over
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
with arbitrary gauge
transformations as morphisms.
The 2-group structure on

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{G}_{f})=\coprod_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}\,{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{G}_{f})_{|v}

(6.31)

from Theorem
5.57
takes the form

\displaystyle(g_{i},v)\otimes(g^{\prime}_{i},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big((\tau_{v^{\prime}}^{*}g_{i})\,g_{i}^{\prime},v+v^{\prime}\big)\ ,

(6.32)

\displaystyle(h,v)\otimes(h^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big((\tau_{v^{\prime}}^{*}h)\,h^{\prime},v+v^{\prime}\big)\ ,

(6.33)

fitting into the 2-group extension from Theorem
5.63
:

\displaystyle 1\longrightarrow\mathrm{HLBdl}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\longrightarrow\text{Sym}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}}(\mathcal{G}_{f})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\mathtt{t}}\longrightarrow 1\ .

(6.34)

As in the case of line bundles from Section
6.1
, a connection
(A,B)
on
\mathcal{G}_{f}
induces a section of the
extension (
6.34
).
A connection on
\mathcal{G}_{f}
is described by a 2-form
B\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and a 1-form

A\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d})
satisfying
